If one of my coworkers questioned my plan or told me I needed to slow down, I’d snap at him. I'd get mad way harder than made any sense for the situation. I just didn't want anyone in my way.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever feel like your thoughts were not your own or that you were hearing things that other people couldn't hear during this time?\n\nPatient: No, I never felt anything like that. My thoughts were definitely mine, they were just... moving really fast. And I never heard any voices or anything that wasn't there. It was just me being really intense and snappy, not like I was losing touch with reality or anything.\n\nDoctor: How long did you feel really fired up and on top of the world before you started feeling down?\n\nPatient: That fired-up feeling... it started right around when I decided to remake myself for the park pitch. I'd say that overconfident stretch lasted for about two weeks. Then, yeah, I just crashed.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience any unusual perceptions or hear voices when you started feeling down?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't heard any voices or seen anything weird since I've been down. It's just... I just feel really low and empty. I mean, my thoughts are just... they're just really dark and negative. I don't think anything crazy or unrealistic. It's more just me thinking about how pointless everything is right now, or how tired I am. But nothing strange like what you're asking about.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience both the fired-up feeling and the down feeling at the same time, even if it was just for a short period?\n\nPatient: No, not at the same time. It was... it was pretty much one after the other. I had that two-week stretch where I was just going non-stop, totally wired about the park pitch. And then it was like hitting a wall. The energy just shut off, and the crash hit hard. But they didn't overlap or anything. Just... up, and then straight down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had other times in your life where you felt extremely fired up or down like this, before the recent episode?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I've had normal days where I'm in a good mood, or just tired from working out in the sun all week, but... nothing like this. This is the first time I've ever been that wired, and definitely the first time I've crashed this hard. So, no, never before this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt as low and empty as you do now at any other point in your life before this recent episode?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... It's just that this recent crash, after being so wired, is definitely the lowest and emptiest I've ever felt. It's the worst it's gotten. But yeah, the down feeling itself... that's been around for about a year.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other episodes like this in the past, where you felt extremely fired up followed by a crash?\n\nPatient: No, like I was saying a minute ago, this is really the only time it's happened. I've never had that... that fired-up feeling before this recent thing with the park pitch. So there was never a time where I crashed after being wired like that, either. It's just been this one time.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any kind of pattern or cycle to your periods of feeling fired up and then crashing into feeling down?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed any pattern... because, like I said, there hasn't been a cycle. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF31.0, F31.1, F31.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... what really brought me in is that things just kind of got out of hand at work. I went from just being a regular crew guy doing landscaping to suddenly trying to run three different bids at once. I was calling city officials, trying to line up all these plant suppliers, and... I just kept messaging everybody I knew about this huge park idea I had. It was like I just couldn't shut it off, you know? I was just going and going.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down about all this, or has it all been more exciting and overwhelming?\n\nPatient: Well, it was exciting at first, but... lately, yeah, I've been feeling pretty down. I just don't really care about much right now. Like, usually I clear my head by going hiking and taking pictures of creek beds and trees and stuff. But lately... I just leave the camera in the truck. I don't even care if the light is perfect or not anymore. I just don't have the interest to even try.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel unusually happy or on top of the world at any point while you were working on those bids and your park idea?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I did. Before I crashed like this, I felt really fired up. It was like I was almost bigger than myself, you know? Like I could just take it all on. \n\nBut it wasn't just being happy. If one of my coworkers questioned my plan or told me I needed to slow down, I’d snap at him. I'd get mad way harder than made any sense for the situation. I just didn't want anyone in my way.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever feel like your thoughts were not your own or that you were hearing things that other people couldn't hear during this time?\n\nPatient: No, I never felt anything like that. My thoughts were definitely mine, they were just... moving really fast. And I never heard any voices or anything that wasn't there. It was just me being really intense and snappy, not like I was losing touch with reality or anything.\n\nDoctor: How long did you feel really fired up and on top of the world before you started feeling down?\n\nPatient: That fired-up feeling... it started right around when I decided to remake myself for the park pitch. I'd say that overconfident stretch lasted for about two weeks. Then, yeah, I just crashed.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience any unusual perceptions or hear voices when you started feeling down?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't heard any voices or seen anything weird since I've been down. It's just... I just feel really low and empty. But my mind isn't playing tricks on me or anything.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any thoughts that seemed strange or unrealistic when you were feeling down?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my thoughts are just... they're just really dark and negative. I don't think anything crazy or unrealistic. It's more just me thinking about how pointless everything is right now, or how tired I am. But nothing strange like what you're asking about.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience both the fired-up feeling and the down feeling at the same time, even if it was just for a short period?\n\nPatient: No, not at the same time. It was... it was pretty much one after the other. I had that two-week stretch where I was just going non-stop, totally wired about the park pitch. And then it was like hitting a wall. The energy just shut off, and the crash hit hard. But they didn't overlap or anything. Just... up, and then straight down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had other times in your life where you felt extremely fired up or down like this, before the recent episode?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I've had normal days where I'm in a good mood, or just tired from working out in the sun all week, but... nothing like this. This is the first time I've ever been that wired, and definitely the first time I've crashed this hard. So, no, never before this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt as low and empty as you do now at any other point in your life before this recent episode?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... I guess I've actually been feeling down for a while now. Like, this whole depressive thing has been hanging over me for about a year, maybe? It's just that this recent crash, after being so wired, is definitely the lowest and emptiest I've ever felt. It's the worst it's gotten. But yeah, the down feeling itself... that's been around for about a year.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other episodes like this in the past, where you felt extremely fired up followed by a crash?\n\nPatient: No, like I was saying a minute ago, this is really the only time it's happened. I've never had that... that fired-up feeling before this recent thing with the park pitch. So there was never a time where I crashed after being wired like that, either. It's just been this one time.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any kind of pattern or cycle to your periods of feeling fired up and then crashing into feeling down?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed any pattern... because, like I said, there hasn't been a cycle. This is the first time I've ever gotten fired up like that, and then crashed. So there's no pattern to it, just this one time.\n\nDoctor: Have your periods of feeling down always started after a period of feeling fired up?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Um... like I was saying, I've been feeling down for about a year now. That was already happening way before this recent fired-up thing. Since I've only ever felt that wired this one time... the low feeling definitely didn't start because of it. It was already there long before.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling down and empty like you are now?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned a minute ago, it's been about a year. That heavy feeling has just been hanging over me for that whole time. But... since I came down from being so fired up, this latest crash is definitely the worst it's gotten so far.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Before I crashed like this, I felt really fired up. It was like I was almost bigger than myself, you know? Like I could just take it all on. \n\nBut it wasn't just being happy. If one of my coworkers questioned my plan or told me I needed to slow down, I’d snap at him. I'd get mad way harder than made any sense for the situation. I just didn't want anyone in my way.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever feel like your thoughts were not your own or that you were hearing things that other people couldn't hear during this time?\n\nPatient: No, I never felt anything like that. My thoughts were definitely mine, they were just... moving really fast. And I never heard any voices or anything that wasn't there. It was just me being really intense and snappy, not like I was losing touch with reality or anything.\n\nDoctor: How long did you feel really fired up and on top of the world before you started feeling down?\n\nPatient: That fired-up feeling... it started right around when I decided to remake myself for the park pitch. I'd say that overconfident stretch lasted for about two weeks. Then, yeah, I just crashed.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience any unusual perceptions or hear voices when you started feeling down?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't heard any voices or seen anything weird since I've been down. It's just... I just feel really low and empty. But my mind isn't playing tricks on me or anything.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any thoughts that seemed strange or unrealistic when you were feeling down?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my thoughts are just... they're just really dark and negative. I don't think anything crazy or unrealistic. It's more just me thinking about how pointless everything is right now, or how tired I am. But nothing strange like what you're asking about.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience both the fired-up feeling and the down feeling at the same time, even if it was just for a short period?\n\nPatient: No, not at the same time. It was... it was pretty much one after the other. I had that two-week stretch where I was just going non-stop, totally wired about the park pitch. And then it was like hitting a wall. The energy just shut off, and the crash hit hard. But they didn't overlap or anything. Just... up, and then straight down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had other times in your life where you felt extremely fired up or down like this, before the recent episode?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I've had normal days where I'm in a good mood, or just tired from working out in the sun all week, but... nothing like this. This is the first time I've ever been that wired, and definitely the first time I've crashed this hard. So, no, never before this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt as low and empty as you do now at any other point in your life before this recent episode?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... It's just that this recent crash, after being so wired, is definitely the lowest and emptiest I've ever felt. It's the worst it's gotten. But yeah, the down feeling itself... that's been around for about a year.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other episodes like this in the past, where you felt extremely fired up followed by a crash?\n\nPatient: No, like I was saying a minute ago, this is really the only time it's happened. It was just me being really intense and snappy, not like I was losing touch with reality or anything.\n\nDoctor: How long did you feel really fired up and on top of the world before you started feeling down?\n\nPatient: That fired-up feeling... it started right around when I decided to remake myself for the park pitch. I'd say that overconfident stretch lasted for about two weeks. Then, yeah, I just crashed.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience any unusual perceptions or hear voices when you started feeling down?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't heard any voices or seen anything weird since I've been down. It's just... I just feel really low and empty. But my mind isn't playing tricks on me or anything.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any thoughts that seemed strange or unrealistic when you were feeling down?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my thoughts are just... they're just really dark and negative. I don't think anything crazy or unrealistic. It's more just me thinking about how pointless everything is right now, or how tired I am. But nothing strange like what you're asking about.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience both the fired-up feeling and the down feeling at the same time, even if it was just for a short period?\n\nPatient: No, not at the same time. It was... it was pretty much one after the other. I had that two-week stretch where I was just going non-stop, totally wired about the park pitch. And then it was like hitting a wall. The energy just shut off, and the crash hit hard. But they didn't overlap or anything. Just... up, and then straight down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had other times in your life where you felt extremely fired up or down like this, before the recent episode?\n\nPatient: No, not really. This is the first time I've ever been that wired, and definitely the first time I've crashed this hard. It's just that this recent crash, after being so wired, is definitely the lowest and emptiest I've ever felt. It's the worst it's gotten. It was just me being really intense and snappy, not like I was losing touch with reality or anything.\n\nDoctor: How long did you feel really fired up and on top of the world before you started feeling down?\n\nPatient: That fired-up feeling... it started right around when I decided to remake myself for the park pitch. I'd say that overconfident stretch lasted for about two weeks. Then, yeah, I just crashed.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience any unusual perceptions or hear voices when you started feeling down?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't heard any voices or seen anything weird since I've been down. It's just... I just feel really low and empty. But my mind isn't playing tricks on me or anything.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any thoughts that seemed strange or unrealistic when you were feeling down?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my thoughts are just... they're just really dark and negative. I don't think anything crazy or unrealistic. It's more just me thinking about how pointless everything is right now, or how tired I am. But nothing strange like what you're asking about.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience both the fired-up feeling and the down feeling at the same time, even if it was just for a short period?\n\nPatient: No, not at the same time. It was... it was pretty much one after the other. I had that two-week stretch where I was just going non-stop, totally wired about the park pitch. And then it was like hitting a wall. The energy just shut off, and the crash hit hard. But they didn't overlap or anything. Just... up, and then straight down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had other times in your life where you felt extremely fired up or down like this, before the recent episode?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I've had normal days where I'm in a good mood, or just tired from working out in the sun all week, but... nothing like this. This is the first time I've ever been that wired, and definitely the first time I've crashed this hard. So, no, never before this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt as low and empty as you do now at any other point in your life before this recent episode?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... I guess I've actually been feeling down for a while now. Like, this whole depressive thing has been hanging over me for about a year, maybe? It's just that this recent crash, after being so wired, is definitely the lowest and emptiest I've ever felt. It's the worst it's gotten. But yeah, the down feeling itself... that's been around for about a year.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other episodes like this in the past, where you felt extremely fired up followed by a crash?\n\nPatient: No, like I was saying a minute ago, this is really the only time it's happened. I've never had that... that fired-up feeling before this recent thing with the park pitch. So there was never a time where I crashed after being wired like that, either. It's just been this one time.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any kind of pattern or cycle to your periods of feeling fired up and then crashing into feeling down?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed any pattern... because, like I said, there hasn't been a cycle. This is the first time I've ever gotten fired up like that, and then crashed. So there's no pattern to it, just this one time.\n\nDoctor: Have your periods of feeling down always started after a period of feeling fired up?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Um... like I was saying, I've been feeling down for about a year now. That was already happening way before this recent fired-up thing. Since I've only ever felt that wired this one time... the low feeling definitely didn't start because of it. It was already there long before.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling down and empty like you are now?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned a minute ago, it's been about a year. That heavy feeling has just been hanging over me for that whole time. But... since I came down from being so fired up, this latest crash is definitely the worst it's gotten so far.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of feeling fired up like that without feeling down afterwards?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Like I said, this is the first time I've ever felt fired up like that in my whole life. So there's never been another time, with or without a crash afterwards. It's just been this one time.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling down and empty since the crash after being fired up?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure how long it's been since the crash, to be honest. Um... I haven't really been keeping track of the days. When you feel this heavy, time just kind of blends together. So I don't have an exact number for you... just that it's been a little while since that fired-up feeling stopped and I hit this bottom.\n\nDoctor: How many weeks have you been feeling down and empty since the crash after being fired up?\n\nPatient: Like I was just saying, I really don't know. I haven't been counting the weeks. It's just been... a while since I crashed. I wish I could give you an exact number, but my head's just too cloudy to keep track of time like that right now.\n\nDoctor: How many weeks have you been feeling down and empty since you crashed after being fired up about the park pitch?\n\nPatient: Look, I... I really don't know what else to tell you. Like I just said a second ago, I don't know how many weeks it's been. I'm not trying to be difficult, I just genuinely haven't been keeping track of the calendar since that park pitch thing ended and I crashed. I just know it's been a while. That's really all I can give you.\n\nDoctor: How many weeks have you been feeling down and empty since you first started feeling that way, not just since the crash after the park pitch?\n\nPatient: I guess... if we're talking about the whole thing, the depression has been hanging over me for about a year now. It's just that this latest crash after the pitch... it's the worst it's gotten. But yeah, the overall heavy feeling has been there for roughly a year.\n\nDoctor: How many weeks have you been feeling down and empty since you crashed after being fired up about the park pitch?\n\nPatient: I... Doc, I really don't know how else to say it. You keep asking me for a number of weeks since the crash, and I just don't have one. I'm not keeping track of time like that. I can't give you a specific number of weeks.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
